## Derived State Systems

`DerivedStateDirty` is the dirty-bit gate for built-in systems. Hierarchy mutations mark hierarchy, active state, world transforms, node cache, and render extract dirty. Active changes mark active state, node cache, and render extract dirty. Transform changes mark world transforms, node cache, and render extract dirty. Mobility and render-layer updates mark the node cache and render extract dirty without forcing transform propagation.

M2 makes scene mutators dirty-only. Spawn, parent changes, transform setters, active setters, fixed component setters, typed API writes, project record insertion, and property writes no longer eagerly flush pending scene systems. `World::new()` and project-load normalization still call the explicit `flush_scene_systems_now()` boundary so bootstrapped and loaded worlds start from coherent cached state.

`World::run_internal_scene_system` consumes one `InternalSceneSystem` and clears only that system's dirty bit after it runs. `flush_pending_scene_systems()` and `flush_scene_systems_now()` are explicit flush boundaries; scheduled ticks run native systems through `WorldDriver` and `SceneScheduleRunner` rather than hiding propagation inside every mutator.

Direct reads have a split contract during dirty windows. `World::local_transform()` returns the current local component by value without node projection; `World::world_transform()` and `World::active_in_hierarchy()` project current values from authoritative local transform, hierarchy, and active-self maps without clearing dirty bits. `World::world_matrix()` and `World::nodes()` remain schedule-maintained cached outputs. `World::node_records()` and owned `World::find_node()` project current direct records for callers that need the complete up-to-date node data before `PostUpdate` refreshes the cache; scalar hot paths must use the narrow read APIs.

The `RenderExtractPrepare` built-in flushes pending hierarchy/active/transform/node-cache state before the frame extract is built, then clears the render-extract dirty bit. M3 makes the prepared scene frame extract canonical: `World` now fills `RenderFrameExtract` sections directly after the dirty-state flush, while `SceneViewportRenderPacket` and `RenderFrameExtract::from_snapshot(...)` remain limited to snapshot preview, roundtrip, and legacy adapter callers.

Fresh 2026-06-06 render-extract camera-layer light evidence: `World` now applies the effective camera `RenderLayerSet` to extracted ambient, directional, point, rect, and spot lights before building `LightingExtract` or `SceneViewportRenderPacket` light snapshots. Meshes, sprites, visibility, and lights therefore share one camera render-layer culling boundary. Ambient light snapshots still omit `node_id`, so their owning entity is filtered before projection. This is camera culling only and does not add per-light object masks, cascades, point/spot shadow rendering, or new light-layer authoring APIs. Static validation covered scoped rustfmt, source scans for camera-layered collectors and no stale no-argument light collector calls, direct render-extract tests for every light family plus explicit camera override, conflict-marker/trailing-whitespace scans, and diff hygiene; focused Cargo remains deferred while external workspace/runtime Cargo and Rust compiler lanes are active.

## Validation Scope

The M1 tests cover slot reuse with stale generation rejection, stable ID to internal location mapping in `World`, skipped registry serialization with roundtrip rebuild, table swap-remove behavior, sparse-set removal behavior, typed resource/event behavior, schedule stage order, duplicate/blank system rejection, plugin hook mutation ordering, render-extract stage flushing, and existing scene roundtrip paths.

The typed ECS M2 tests cover tuple bundle spawn, typed insert/get/get_mut/remove, typed resource registration/replacement, fixed component setter convergence through `ComponentId`, dynamic plugin component presence through `ComponentId`, and runtime-only typed ECS state staying out of scene project serialization.

The typed ECS M3 query tests cover required component reads, optional component reads, stable `EntityId` query items, `With<T>` and `Without<T>` filters, single-component mutable iteration, fixed scene component queries, access conflict detection, filter-proven disjointness, and duplicate mutable access rejection.

The typed ECS system-param/change-detection tests cover deferred command visibility before/after `World::apply_deferred()`, entity command builder ordering, tuple system params up to eight items, tuple params combining query/resource/commands access, optional and required resource params, `ParamSet` segmented conflicting access up to eight items, event reader/writer current/next queues, counted event-writer batches that remain in the next queue until event update, event reader cursor retention and clearing across system runs, persistent local params, scheduled native-system local state across stage runs, explicit scheduled native `Added<T>`/`Changed<T>` windows, scheduled native message and removed-component reader cursors, scheduled native deferred command invisibility before the producer returns, later ordered scheduled native visibility after deferred flush, generated native `ApplyDeferred` step visibility in the sorted schedule plan, generated native `ApplyDeferred` barrier visibility in the conflict graph and conservative batch plan, `Ref<T>`/`Mut<T>` query wrappers, `Added<T>` windows, `Changed<T>` windows after direct mutable component access, cached and cached-direct system query iteration, cache-backed system mutable query iteration, single-result helpers, targeted get helpers, many-get helpers, unique mutable many-get helpers, read-only projection for mutable query data including cached-direct `Mut<T>` tick metadata, read-only iter-many helpers including cached and cached-direct run-window variants, read-only combination helpers with cached run-window filters, mutable combination helpers with fetch-next alias discipline, mutable iter-many helpers with fetch-next alias discipline and run-window filters, mutable get/many-get helpers with alias rejection, count/empty/contains helpers against run-window filters, removed-component readers, and explicit `ApplyDeferred` schedule flushing.

The scheduled-native tests also include a source guard for the M5 schedule-runner hot path: `WorldDriver` must snapshot the maintained schedule-stage plan and stage-indexed scene runtime hook plan once per tick, `SceneScheduleRunner` must receive borrowed descriptor/hook slices, `ScheduledSceneStep::iter_sorted_for_stage(...)` must stay the runner's merge entry, and the removed owned `ScheduledSceneStep::sorted_for_stage(...)` helper must not return. `ecs_schedule` also verifies that the maintained schedule-stage plan is refreshed after registration and rebuilt after JSON load.

The M11 query-cache tests in `ecs_query.rs` verify that cached iteration is built once for an existing query, reuses the cache across component value replacement, rebuilds after a matching entity is spawned, rebuilds after a queried component is removed, keeps cached stable and component storage locations aligned with world entity/component rows, moves entity locations across archetype signatures on component add/remove, does not treat optional reads as required archetype membership, preserves write-access component locations during mutable-query cache rebuild, drives mutable `for_each_mut(...)` from the cached structural candidate list, supports five-plus query data/filter tuple shapes, can query `StableEntityLocation`, reports read-only single-query zero/one/many outcomes with `QuerySingleError`, exposes targeted get, many-get, unique many-get, and count/empty/contains helpers over cached and cached-direct candidates, adds normal/cached/cached-direct read-only iter-many helpers, preserves many-get and iter-many input order plus duplicate read-only entity requests, rejects duplicate entity ids for unique many-get helpers, supports mutable targeted get and many-get while rejecting duplicate mutable entities, supports mutable iter-many with cursor-style duplicate entity requests, rejects nonexistent entities for get/contains checks, skips nonexistent entities during read-only and mutable iter-many, and can use `iter_cached_direct(...)` / `single_cached_direct(...)` for read-only `EntityId`, `StableEntityLocation`, `&T`, `Option<&T>`, and `Ref<T>` items over both table and sparse-set storage. The dedicated `ecs_query_many.rs`, `ecs_query_single.rs`, `ecs_query_combinations.rs`, `ecs_system_query_cache.rs`, and `ecs_performance_acceptance.rs` modules keep newer many-query, single-query, combination, system-cache, and hot-path acceptance coverage out of the large legacy query/system files; many-query tests cover mutable-data read-only projection, cached-direct `&mut T` projection, cached-direct `Mut<T>` projection to `Ref<T>`, unique mutable many-get order, mutation, mismatch diagnostics, and system run-window filtering in addition to unique mutable iteration; combination tests cover normal/cached pair and triple groups, `K > N` empty results, `Changed<T>` run-window filtering, and mutable `fetch_next()` pair traversal for both manual `QueryState` and system `Query`; system-cache tests cover default system `Query::iter(...)` cache reuse, dynamic `Changed<T>` filter rechecks, structural cache rebuild after spawn, cached stable-entity index rebuild after component removal, cached iter-many order/duplicate semantics, and the distinction between non-cached `QueryState::iter(...)` and cached/default system iteration carrying cached component locations; performance-acceptance tests cover 128-entity spawn/query reuse, value replacement without cache rebuild, matching spawn rebuild, `Changed<T>` matching only 16 mutated entities without rebuild, and 128 repeated transform projections over pending derived state. The storage tests verify table-row location lookup, table swap-remove row updates, stale table-location rejection, sparse-set location reporting, direct table-row reads, and direct location reads with ticks. The 2026-06-07 component-storage sizing follow-up pre-sizes `remove_entity(...)` and `component_ids_for_entity(...)` result vectors to the number of registered table plus sparse component storages before scanning, so these storage-wide collection paths avoid result-vector growth while keeping sorted `ComponentId` output. The 2026-06-07 component-storage entry-lookup follow-up moves the storage-type and component-type consistency guards to `HashMap::entry(...)`, so insertion validates or records component metadata with a single map probe while preserving storage-mismatch and component-type-mismatch diagnostics. The 2026-06-07 table component row-entry follow-up moves `TableComponentStorage::insert(...)` to the row-index map entry API, so replacing an existing table component and appending a new row both avoid the old lookup-then-insert row-index pattern while preserving table row stability. The 2026-06-07 sparse component entry follow-up moves `SparseComponentStorage::insert(...)` to the sparse-entry map API, so replacing an existing sparse component updates ticks and swaps the stored value without the old insert-then-get-mut follow-up lookup. The 2026-06-07 table mark-changed follow-up reuses the resolved table row index directly when setting component ticks, avoiding the prior row lookup plus second `entries.get_mut(row)` probe during table-backed mutable access. The 2026-06-07 table get-mut follow-up reuses the resolved table row index directly for mutable value downcasts, avoiding the prior row lookup plus checked vector lookup in table-backed `get_mut<T>(...)`. The 2026-06-07 table get follow-up reuses the resolved table row index directly for read-only value downcasts, avoiding the prior row lookup plus checked vector lookup in table-backed `get<T>(...)`. The 2026-06-07 table ticks follow-up reuses the resolved table row index directly for component tick reads, avoiding the prior row lookup plus checked vector lookup in table-backed `ticks(...)`. The 2026-06-07 mutable-at-tick follow-up sends `ComponentStorage::get_mut_at_tick<T>(...)` through one storage-type dispatch and storage-local `get_mut_at_tick(...)` helpers, so table and sparse mutable access set change ticks and downcast through the same resolved row or entry instead of calling public `mark_changed(...)` and then `get_mut(...)` as two separate lookups. The M11 schedule-conflict tests now live in `ecs_schedule/conflict_graph.rs` instead of growing the already-large schedule lifecycle test module; they verify same-stage component read/write conflicts, `With<T>`/`Without<T>` disjointness, stage isolation, resource read/write conflicts, event/message write conflicts, concrete `SystemParamConflictKind` values, conflict lookup through the graph-owned node-index adjacency and node-owned edge-index vectors, conservative batch grouping through the graph-owned node-index adjacency cache, boolean `SystemParamAccess::conflicts_with(...)` gating before detailed graph diagnostics, batch separation at stage boundaries, `JobScheduler` batch execution, missing-task diagnostics, and task-failure diagnostics.

Runtime 03 update: schedule-executor behavior coverage now lives in `ecs_schedule/parallel_executor.rs`. The older `ecs_schedule/conflict_graph.rs` file remains responsible for conflict detection and conservative batch construction only, keeping both test modules below the large-file threshold.

The M5 conflict-graph edge-index guard keeps `conflicts_for(...)` on `conflict_edge_indices_by_node: Vec<Vec<usize>>` plus `node_indices_by_system_id` instead of scanning `self.edges` or maintaining a string-keyed diagnostic edge-index map. Public `systems_conflict(...)`, `conflicts_for(...)`, and `conservative_parallel_batches()` now all enter through node indices, while `conflict_node_adjacency: Vec<Vec<usize>>` plus private `ScheduleParallelBatch` node indices keeps the batching hot path from repeating system-id hash lookups or allocating per-node hash sets. Graph construction discovers node pairs by global left-node order, per-stage right-node buckets, and a per-stage cursor for the current left node, pushes both adjacency directions into sorted vectors, pushes the edge index into the two node-owned diagnostic vectors, and `node_indices_conflict(...)` uses binary search for public/private pair membership checks. Batch construction uses `node_indices_conflict_with_any(...)` plus `sorted_slices_intersect(...)` to compare an active batch's sorted node-index list with the candidate node's sorted adjacency list in one cursor walk. Graph construction also calls the boolean `SystemParamAccess::conflicts_with(...)` gate before building `SystemParamConflictKind` vectors, so the common no-conflict candidate pair pays only sorted intersection checks. The edge vector remains the stable diagnostic record returned by `edges()`.

The transform/active dirty-state M2 tests cover dirty-only mutators, direct read projection without clearing dirty bits, `PostUpdate` cache propagation, `RenderExtract` flushing after parent reorder and active changes, and mobility changes that dirty cached node/render state without forcing transform propagation.

Fresh 2026-06-05/06 M5 scene-hook stage-plan evidence introduced `SceneRuntimeHookSet` and its cached `Arc<SceneRuntimeHookStagePlan>`. The 2026-07-13 Frameworks05 ownership correction moves both into `scene/runtime_hook` and makes `WorldDriver` the executable hook store. The stage-plan rebuild counts hooks per stage, allocates each bucket with exact `Vec::with_capacity(...)` sizing, and distributes hooks in canonical stage/order/id order; the empty default plan still uses empty buckets. `WorldDriver` passes `hooks.hooks_for_stage(stage)` into `SceneScheduleRunner`; `ScheduledSceneStep::iter_sorted_for_stage(...)` asserts that hook/native inputs are already stage-filtered and no longer re-filters hook slices per stage. The old `CoreRuntime`/`CoreHandle` installation and snapshot APIs and plugin hook facade were deleted, so tick execution cannot route executable scene behavior through the kernel or plugin facade.

Fresh 2026-06-05 M5 schedule stage-plan evidence: `Schedule` now owns a crate-private `Arc<SceneScheduleStagePlan>` executor cache. The plan preserves the serialized stage order while grouping internal `SceneSystemDescriptor` values and generated native `ScheduledSceneStep` values by `SystemStage`; registration and deserialization rebuild the cache, and `WorldDriver::tick_level(...)` clones one stable `Arc` snapshot for the tick instead of rebuilding stage buckets. If a native system registers schedule work while another native system is temporarily taken for execution, `Schedule` now marks the executor plan dirty and refreshes after the last taken system is restored; taken native ids remain reserved so duplicate registration cannot hide behind the temporary removal. The 2026-06-08 compact reservation follow-up keeps those temporary taken-native ids in a small `Vec<String>` with direct push and linear membership instead of paying `BTreeSet` node overhead for the normal one-or-few in-flight systems case. The later taken-native restore membership follow-up removes the restored id through first-match `position(...)` plus `swap_remove(...)`; this is safe because the taken-id vector is only a membership guard and sorted restore order is owned by `SceneSystemRegistry::restore_native_system(...)`. The 2026-06-07 internal-bucket sizing follow-up counts internal systems per stage before allocating `internal_systems_by_stage`, so each stage bucket uses exact `Vec::with_capacity(...)` sizing before descriptor clones are distributed in registry order. `SceneScheduleRunner` receives borrowed stage slices and no longer reads the world to rebuild native steps per stage. `ScheduledSceneStep::iter_sorted_for_stage(...)` asserts that internal, native, and hook inputs are stage-specific and merges the already selected slices together. This follows Bevy's split between a graph/configuration surface and executor-facing `SystemSchedule` data while staying inside Zircon's existing `Schedule` serialization contract. Fresh static validation covered rustfmt, source guards for `SceneScheduleStagePlan`, borrowed runner inputs, no flat `world.schedule().systems().to_vec()` snapshot, no per-stage native-step world read, no internal/hook re-filtering in `ScheduledSceneStep`, conflict-marker/trailing-whitespace scans, diff hygiene, and the runtime structure audit. Fresh 2026-06-05 M5 no-allocation scheduled-step merge evidence: `SceneScheduleRunner` now consumes `ScheduledSceneStep::iter_sorted_for_stage(...)` instead of materializing an owned `Vec<ScheduledSceneStep>` on the hot path. `SortedScheduledSceneSteps<'_>` performs a borrowed merge over the already stage-specific internal system slice, native/apply-deferred step slice, and scene-hook slice, selecting by `order`, id, and step-rank. Fresh 2026-07-22 performance audit evidence: `ArchetypeIndex::move_entity(...)` now receives the previous archetype id and table row from `EntityRegistry`. Same-signature component replacement returns that row directly; real signature migration swap-removes the known old row and appends the target row, so neither path linearly scans the archetype entity vector. `ecs_archetype_index_structure.rs` and `ecs_identity_storage.rs` pin the row-authority flow. The broader storage architecture remains open under PERF-MVP-481: current `TableComponentStorage` is still per-component `HashMap<InternalEntity, usize>` plus `Vec<Box<dyn Any>>`, not archetype-owned row-aligned columns, so component membership rebuild, despawn and query retain storage-wide scans/hash/downcast costs. Static source guards, production rustfmt and scoped diff hygiene passed; current-source Cargo and scale counters are pending.
